=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given a string s and a pattern string p, where p contains exactly one '*' character.
The '*' in p can be replaced with any sequence of zero or more characters.
Return true if p can be made a substring of s, and false otherwise.
A substring is a contiguous non-empty sequence of characters within a string.
 
Example 1:

Input: s = "leetcode", p = "ee*e"
Output: true
Explanation:
By replacing the '*' with "tcod", the substring "eetcode" matches the pattern.

Example 2:

Input: s = "car", p = "c*v"
Output: false
Explanation:
There is no substring matching the pattern.

Example 3:

Input: s = "luck", p = "u*"
Output: true
Explanation:
The substrings "u", "uc", and "uck" match the pattern.

 
Constraints:

1 <= s.length <= 50
1 <= p.length <= 50 
s contains only lowercase English letters.
p contains only lowercase English letters and exactly one '*'

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def hasMatch(self, s: str, p: str) -> bool:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's decide final answer: code block with:
```python
class Solution:
    def hasMatch(self, s: str, p: str) -> bool:
        prefix, suffix = p.split('*')
        pos = s.find(prefix)
        if pos == -1:
            return False
        return s.find(suffix, pos + len(prefix)) != -1
```
This is concise. Need maybe use `left, right` not `prefix`? Fine.

If prefix empty and suffix empty, pos=0, find('',0)=0. true.
If prefix non-empty and suffix empty, pos first prefix. find('', pos+len(prefix)) returns pos+len(prefix) if <= len(s). Since prefix occurrence ensures pos+len(prefix) <= len(s). true. Good.

Let's maybe search memory of LeetCode problem "Substring Matching Pattern" (LC 3236? maybe). The official solution might be:
```
class Solution:
    def hasMatch(self, s: str, p: str) -> bool:
        i = p.index('*')
        return p[:i] in s and p[i+1:] in s[s.index(p[:i]) + i:] 
```
Wait there is a nuance: They might do `s.find(p[:i]) != -1 and s.find(p[i+1:], s.find(p[:i]) + i) != -1`. But if prefix empty, `s.index('')` = 0, then `s[s.index('') + 0:]` = s, check suffix in s. Good. If prefix non-empty, `i` is length of prefix (because star at index i), so `s.index(prefix) + i` = pos + len(prefix). Good.

Let's think if there is a possibility that prefix occurs at pos, but suffix occurs after pos+len(prefix), but the substring formed from pos to suffix end is not necessarily contiguous? It is contiguous. Good.

class Solution:
    def hasMatch(self, s: str, p: str) -> bool:
        i = p.index('*')

class Solution:
    def hasMatch(self, s: str, p: str) -> bool:
        prefix, suffix = p.split('*')
        pos = s.find(prefix)
        if pos == -1:
            return False
        return s.find(suffix, pos + len(prefix)) != -1
```
